    window.loadScript = (url, { charset = 'UTF-8', ...attributes } = {}, location = 'head') => {
      const existingScript = document.querySelector('script[src="' + url + '"]');
      if (existingScript) {
        return;
      }

      const script = document.createElement('script');
      script.src = url;
      script.type = 'text/javascript';

      script.async = true;
      script.charset = charset;

      if (attributes) {
        Object.entries(attributes).forEach(([key, value]) => {
          script.setAttribute(key, value);
        });
      }

      if (location === 'head') {
        document.head.appendChild(script);
      } else {
        document.body.appendChild(script);
      }

      return script;
    };

scannable API tokens, enhanced OAuth visibility, and GA for resource-scoped permissions. These tools help developers implement a true least-privilege architecture while protecting against credential leakage.\n&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;Agents let you build software faster than ever, but securing your environment and the code you write â from both mistakes and malice â takes real effort. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/security/threats/owasp-top-10/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Open Web Application Security Project&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (OWASP) details a number of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://genai.owasp.org/resource/agentic-ai-threats-and-mitigations/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;risks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; present in agentic AI systems, including the risk of credential leaks, user impersonation, and elevation of privilege. These risks can result in extreme damage to your environments including denial of service, data loss, or data leaks â which can do untold financial and reputational damage.Â  &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is an identity problem. In modern development, &amp;quot;identities&amp;quot; aren&amp;#39;t just people â they are the agents, scripts, and third-party tools that act on your behalf. To secure these non-human identities, you need to manage their entire lifecycle: ensuring their credentials (tokens) aren&amp;#39;t leaked, seeing which applications have access via OAuth, and narrowing their permissions using granular RBAC.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, we are introducing updates to address these needs: scannable tokens to protect your credentials,&lt;b&gt; &lt;/b&gt;OAuth visibility to manage your principals, and resource-scoped RBAC to fine-tune your policies.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;understanding-identity-principals-credentials-and-policies\&quot;&gt;Understanding identity: Principals, Credentials, and Policies&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#understanding-identity-principals-credentials-and-policies\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;To secure the Internet in an era of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/what-is-agentic-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;autonomous agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, we have to rethink how we handle identity. Whether a request comes from a human developer or an AI agent, every interaction with an API relies on three core pillars:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Principal (The Traveler):&lt;/b&gt; This is the identity itself â the &amp;quot;who.&amp;quot; It might be you logging in via OAuth, or a background agent using an API token to deploy code.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Credential (The Passport):&lt;/b&gt; This is the proof of that identity. In this world, your API token is your passport. If itâs stolen or leaked, anyone can &amp;quot;wear&amp;quot; your identity.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Policy (The Visa):&lt;/b&gt; This defines what that identity is allowed to do. Just because you have a valid passport doesn&amp;#39;t mean you have a visa to enter every country. A policy ensures that even a verified identity can only access the specific resources it needs.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;When these three pillars aren&amp;#39;t managed together, security breaks down. You might have a valid Principal using a stolen Credential, or a legitimate identity with a Policy that is far too broad. &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;leaked-token-detection\&quot;&gt;Leaked token detection&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#leaked-token-detection\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agents and other third-party applications use API tokens to access the Cloudflare API. One of the simplest ways that we see people leaking their secrets is by accidentally pushing them to a public GitHub repository. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.gitguardian.com/files/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-report-2026\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;GitGuardian&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; reports that last year more than 28 million secrets were published to public GitHub repositories, and that AI is causing leaks to happen 5x faster than before.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If an API token is a digital passport, then leaking it on a public repository is like leaving your passport on a park bench. Anyone who finds it can impersonate that identity until the document is canceled. Our partnership with GitHub acts like a global &amp;quot;lost and found&amp;quot; for these credentials. By the time you realize your passport is missing, weâve already identified the document, verified its authenticity via the checksum, and voided it to prevent misuse.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâre partnering with several leading credential scanning tools to help proactively find your leaked tokens and revoke them before they could be used maliciously. We know itâs not a matter of if, but rather when, before you, an employee, or one of your agents makes a mistake and pushes a secret somewhere it shouldnât be.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;github\&quot;&gt;GitHub&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#github\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Weâve partnered with GitHub and are participating in their Secret Scanning program to find your tokens in both public and private repositories. If we are notified that a token has leaked to a public repository, we will automatically revoke the token to prevent it from being used maliciously. For private repositories, GitHub will notify you about any leaked Cloudflare tokens and you can clean these up.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h5 id=\&quot;how-it-works\&quot;&gt;How it works&lt;/h5&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-it-works\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Weâve shared the new token formats (below!) with GitHub, and they now scan for them on every commit. If they find something that looks like a leaked Cloudflare token, they verify the token is real (using the checksum), send us a webhook to revoke it, and then we notify you via email so you can generate a new one in Dashboard settings.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This means we plug the hole as soon as itâs found. By the time you realize you made a mistake, we&amp;#39;ve already fixed it.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We hope this is the kind of feature you donât need to use, but our partners are on the lookout for leaks to help keep you secure.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;cloudflare-one\&quot;&gt;Cloudflare One&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#cloudflare-one\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cloudflare One customers are also protected from these leaks. By configuring the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/data-loss-prevention/dlp-profiles/predefined-profiles/#credentials-and-secrets\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Credentials and Secrets&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; DLP profile, organizations can activate prevention everywhere a credential can travel:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Network Traffic (&lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/products/gateway/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Gateway&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;b&gt;):&lt;/b&gt; Apply these entries to a policy to detect and block Cloudflare API tokens moving across your network. A token in a file upload, an outbound request, or a download is stopped before it reaches its destination.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Outbound Email (&lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/products/email-security/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Email Security&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;b&gt;):&lt;/b&gt; Microsoft 365 customers can extend this same prevention to Outlook. The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/email-security/outbound-dlp/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;DLP Assist&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; add-in scans messages before delivery, catching a token before itâs sent externally.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Data at Rest (&lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/products/casb/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare AI Gateway&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; integrates with the same DLP profiles to scan and block both incoming prompts and outgoing AI model responses in real time.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;other-credential-scanners\&quot;&gt;Other credential scanners&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#other-credential-scanners\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; These automated security tools scan your code repositories looking for exposed credentials like API keys, tokens or passwords. The âcfâ prefix makes Cloudflare tokens instantly recognizable with greater confidence, and the checksum makes it easy for tools to statically validate them. Your existing tokens will continue to work, but every new token you generate will use the scannable format so itâs easily detected with high confidence.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Credential Type&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;What it&amp;#39;s for&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;New Format&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;User API Key&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Legacy global API key tied to your user account (full access)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfk_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;User API Token&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Scoped token you create for specific permissions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfut_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Account API Token&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Token owned by the account (not a specific user)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfat_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;getting-started\&quot;&gt;Getting started&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#getting-started\&quot; Both require clear visibility to prevent unauthorized access and ensure you know exactly who â or what â has access to your data.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;improving-the-oauth-consent-experience\&quot;&gt;Improving the OAuth consent experience&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#improving-the-oauth-consent-experience\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When you connect third-party applications like Wrangler to your Cloudflare Account using OAuth, you&amp;#39;re granting that application access to your accountâs data. Understanding these scopes before you grant access helps you maintain least-privilege. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We also added a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dash.cloudflare.com/profile/access-management/authorization\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Connected Applications&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; experience so you can see which applications have access to which accounts, what scopes/permissions are associated with that application, and easily revoke that access as needed.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5Aiu82urkjaL9SWpZUBgNi/827cf38aa655d4094de1895d07f51137/BLOG-3216_5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3216 5\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1530\&quot; height=\&quot;527\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;getting-started\&quot;&gt;Getting started&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#getting-started\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The OAuth consent and revocation improvements are available now. Check which apps currently have access to your accounts by visiting My Profile &amp;gt; Access Management &amp;gt; Connected Applications.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For developers building integrations with Cloudflare, keep an eye on the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Changelog&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for more announcements around how you can register your own OAuth apps soon!Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;fine-grained-resource-level-permissioning\&quot;&gt;Fine-grained resource-level permissioningÂ &lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#fine-grained-resource-level-permissioning\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;If the token is the passport, then resource-scoped permissions are the visas inside it. Having a valid passport gets you through the front door, but it shouldn&amp;#39;t give you access to every room in the building. By narrowing the scope to specific resources â like a single Load Balancer pool or a specific Gateway policy â you are ensuring that even if an identity is verified, it only has the &amp;quot;visa&amp;quot; to go where itâs strictly necessary.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Last year, we &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog/post/2025-10-01-fine-grained-permissioning-beta/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;announced&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; support for resource scoped permissions in Cloudflareâs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/access-management/role-based-access-control-rbac/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;role-based access control (RBAC)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; system for several of our Zero Trust products. This enables you to right size permissions for both users and agents to minimize security risks. Weâve expanded this capability to several new resources-level permissions. The resource scope is now supported for:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Applications&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Identity Providers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Policies&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Service Tokens&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Targets&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâve also completely overhauled the API Token creation experience, making it easier for customers to provision and manage Account API Tokens right from the Cloudflare Dashboard. &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2XSjOtE46g8iVNN7QNzoDI/2f2280b106da9ebc9f3959d5300a4241/account_owned_token_gif.gif\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When you add a member to your Cloudflare account or create an API Token, you typically assign that principal a policy. A Permission Policy is what gives a principal permission to take an action, whether thatâs managing Cloudflare One Access Applications, or DNS Records. Without a policy, a principal can authenticate, but they are unauthorized to do any actions within an account.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Policies are made up of three components: a Principal, a Role, and a Scope. The Principal is who or what you&amp;#39;re granting access to, whether that&amp;#39;s a human user, a Non-Human Identity (NHI) like an API Token, or increasingly, an Agent acting on behalf of a user. The Role defines what actions they&amp;#39;re permitted to take. The Scope determines where those permissions apply, and historically, that&amp;#39;s been restricted to the entire account, or individual zones.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;new-permission-roles\&quot;&gt;New permission roles&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#new-permission-roles\&quot; By refining how we manage permissions and credentials, developers and enterprises can have greater confidence in their security posture across the users, apps, agents, and scripts that access Cloudflare. Least privilege isnât a new concept, and for enterprises, itâs never been optional. Whether a human administrator is managing a zone or an agent is programmatically deploying a Worker, the expectation is the same, they should only be authorized to do the job it was given, and nothing else.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Following todayâs announcement, we recommend customers:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Review your &lt;a These risks can result in extreme damage to your environments including denial of service, data loss, or data leaks â which can do untold financial and reputational damage.Â  &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is an identity problem. In modern development, &amp;quot;identities&amp;quot; aren&amp;#39;t just people â they are the agents, scripts, and third-party tools that act on your behalf. To secure these non-human identities, you need to manage their entire lifecycle: ensuring their credentials (tokens) aren&amp;#39;t leaked, seeing which applications have access via OAuth, and narrowing their permissions using granular RBAC.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, we are introducing updates to address these needs: scannable tokens to protect your credentials,&lt;b&gt; &lt;/b&gt;OAuth visibility to manage your principals, and resource-scoped RBAC to fine-tune your policies.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;understanding-identity-principals-credentials-and-policies\&quot;&gt;Understanding identity: Principals, Credentials, and Policies&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#understanding-identity-principals-credentials-and-policies\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;To secure the Internet in an era of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/what-is-agentic-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;autonomous agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, we have to rethink how we handle identity. Whether a request comes from a human developer or an AI agent, every interaction with an API relies on three core pillars:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Principal (The Traveler):&lt;/b&gt; This is the identity itself â the &amp;quot;who.&amp;quot; It might be you logging in via OAuth, or a background agent using an API token to deploy code.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Credential (The Passport):&lt;/b&gt; This is the proof of that identity. In this world, your API token is your passport. If itâs stolen or leaked, anyone can &amp;quot;wear&amp;quot; your identity.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Policy (The Visa):&lt;/b&gt; This defines what that identity is allowed to do. Just because you have a valid passport doesn&amp;#39;t mean you have a visa to enter every country. A policy ensures that even a verified identity can only access the specific resources it needs.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;When these three pillars aren&amp;#39;t managed together, security breaks down. A token in a file upload, an outbound request, or a download is stopped before it reaches its destination.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Outbound Email (&lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/products/email-security/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Email Security&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;b&gt;):&lt;/b&gt; Microsoft 365 customers can extend this same prevention to Outlook. The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/email-security/outbound-dlp/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;DLP Assist&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; These automated security tools scan your code repositories looking for exposed credentials like API keys, tokens or passwords. The âcfâ prefix makes Cloudflare tokens instantly recognizable with greater confidence, and the checksum makes it easy for tools to statically validate them. Your existing tokens will continue to work, but every new token you generate will use the scannable format so itâs easily detected with high confidence.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Credential Type&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;What it&amp;#39;s for&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;New Format&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;User API Key&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Legacy global API key tied to your user account (full access)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfk_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;User API Token&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Scoped token you create for specific permissions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfut_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Account API Token&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Token owned by the account (not a specific user)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfat_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;getting-started\&quot;&gt;Getting started&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#getting-started\&quot; Having a valid passport gets you through the front door, but it shouldn&amp;#39;t give you access to every room in the building. By narrowing the scope to specific resources â like a single Load Balancer pool or a specific Gateway policy â you are ensuring that even if an identity is verified, it only has the &amp;quot;visa&amp;quot; to go where itâs strictly necessary.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Last year, we &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog/post/2025-10-01-fine-grained-permissioning-beta/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;announced&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; support for resource scoped permissions in Cloudflareâs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/access-management/role-based-access-control-rbac/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;role-based access control (RBAC)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; system for several of our Zero Trust products. This enables you to right size permissions for both users and agents to minimize security risks. Weâve expanded this capability to several new resources-level permissions. The resource scope is now supported for:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Applications&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Identity Providers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Policies&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Service Tokens&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Targets&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâve also completely overhauled the API Token creation experience, making it easier for customers to provision and manage Account API Tokens right from the Cloudflare Dashboard. &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2XSjOtE46g8iVNN7QNzoDI/2f2280b106da9ebc9f3959d5300a4241/account_owned_token_gif.gif\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When you add a member to your Cloudflare account or create an API Token, you typically assign that principal a policy. A Permission Policy is what gives a principal permission to take an action, whether thatâs managing Cloudflare One Access Applications, or DNS Records. Without a policy, a principal can authenticate, but they are unauthorized to do any actions within an account.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Policies are made up of three components: a Principal, a Role, and a Scope. The Principal is who or what you&amp;#39;re granting access to, whether that&amp;#39;s a human user, a Non-Human Identity (NHI) like an API Token, or increasingly, an Agent acting on behalf of a user. The Role defines what actions they&amp;#39;re permitted to take. The Scope determines where those permissions apply, and historically, that&amp;#39;s been restricted to the entire account, or individual zones.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;new-permission-roles\&quot;&gt;New permission roles&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#new-permission-roles\&quot; To do that, they need a browser. They need to navigate sites, read pages, fill forms, extract data, and take screenshots. They need to observe whether things are working as expected, with a way for their humans to step in if needed. And they need to do all of this at scale.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, weâre renaming Browser Rendering to &lt;b&gt;Browser Run&lt;/b&gt;, and shipping key features that make it &lt;i&gt;the&lt;/i&gt; browser for &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/what-is-agentic-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AI agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. The name Browser Rendering never fully captured what the product does. Browser Run lets you run full browser sessions on Cloudflare&amp;#39;s global network, drive them with code or AI, record and replay sessions, crawl pages for content, debug in real time, and let humans intervene when your agent needs help.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Hereâs whatâs new:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Live View&lt;/b&gt;: see what your agent sees and is doing, in real time. Know instantly if things are working, and when theyâre not, see exactly why.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Human in the Loop&lt;/b&gt;: when your agent hits a snag like a login page or unexpected edge case, it can hand off to a human instead of failing. The human steps in, resolves, then hands back control.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP) Endpoint&lt;/b&gt;: the Chrome DevTools Protocol is how agents control browsers. Browser Run now exposes it directly, so agents get maximum control over the browser and existing CDP scripts work on Cloudflare.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;MCP Client Support:&lt;/b&gt; AI coding agents like Claude Desktop, Cursor, and OpenCode can now use Browser Run as their remote browser.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;WebMCP Support&lt;/b&gt;: agents will outnumber humans using the web. WebMCP allows websites to declare what actions are available for agents to discover and call, making navigation more reliable.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Session Recordings&lt;/b&gt;: capture every browser session for debugging purposes. When something goes wrong, you have the full recording with DOM changes, user interactions, and page navigation.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Higher limits&lt;/b&gt;: run more tasks at once with 120 concurrent browsers, up from 30.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;div style=\&quot;position: relative; padding-top: 54.98194945848375%;\&quot;&gt;\n  &lt;iframe\n    &lt;td class=\&quot;tg-1wig\&quot;&gt;&lt;span style=\&quot;font-weight:700;font-style:normal;text-decoration:none;color:#000;background-color:transparent\&quot;&gt;Live View (new)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span style=\&quot;font-weight:400;font-style:normal;text-decoration:none;color:#000;background-color:transparent\&quot;&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span &lt;td class=\&quot;tg-0lax\&quot;&gt;&lt;span style=\&quot;font-weight:400;font-style:normal;text-decoration:none;color:#000;background-color:transparent\&quot;&gt;4) Human intervention&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/td&gt;\n    &lt;td class=\&quot;tg-1wig\&quot;&gt;&lt;span &lt;td class=\&quot;tg-0lax\&quot;&gt;&lt;span style=\&quot;font-weight:400;font-style:normal;text-decoration:none;color:#000;background-color:transparent\&quot;&gt;5) Scale&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/td&gt;\n    &lt;td class=\&quot;tg-0lax\&quot;&gt;&lt;span style=\&quot;font-weight:400;font-style:normal;text-decoration:none;color:#000;background-color:transparent\&quot;&gt;10 requests/second for Quick Actions, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span style=\&quot;font-weight:700;font-style:normal;text-decoration:none;color:#000;background-color:transparent\&quot;&gt;120 concurrent browsers (4x increase)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/td&gt;\n  &lt;/tr&gt;\n&lt;/tbody&gt;&lt;/table&gt;&lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;First, an agent needs a browser. With Browser Run, agents can spin up a headless Chrome instance on Cloudflareâs global network, on demand. No infrastructure to manage, no Chrome versions to maintain. Browser sessions open near users for low latency, and scale up and down as needed. Pair Browser Run with the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/api-reference/browse-the-web/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents SDK&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to build long-running agents that browse the web, remember everything, and act on their own.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;2-take-actions\&quot;&gt;2) Take actions&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#2-take-actions\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Once your agent has a browser, it needs ways to control it. Browser Run supports multiple approaches: new low-level protocol access with the Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP) and WebMCP, in addition to existing higher-level automation using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/puppeteer/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Puppeteer&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/playwright/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Playwright&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Quick Actions&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for simple tasks. Letâs look at the details.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;chrome-devtools-protocol-cdp-endpoint\&quot;&gt;Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP) endpoint&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#chrome-devtools-protocol-cdp-endpoint\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://chromedevtools.github.io/devtools-protocol/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is the low-level protocol that powers browser automation. Exposing CDP directly means the growing ecosystem of agent tools and existing CDP automation scripts can use Browser Run. When you open Chrome DevTools and inspect a page, CDP is what&amp;#39;s running underneath. Puppeteer, Playwright, and most agent frameworks are built on top of it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Every way that you have been using Browser Run has actually been through CDP already. Whatâs new is that we&amp;#39;re now &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/cdp/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;exposing CDP directly&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; as an endpoint. This matters for agents because CDP gives agents the most control possible over the browser. Agent frameworks already speak CDP natively, and can now connect to Browser Run directly. CDP also unlocks browser actions that aren&amp;#39;t available through Puppeteer or Playwright, like JavaScript debugging. And because you&amp;#39;re working with raw CDP messages instead of going through higher-level libraries, you can pass messages directly to models for more token-efficient browser control.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you already have CDP automation scripts running against self-hosted Chrome, they work on Browser Run with a one-line config change. Point your WebSocket URL at Browser Run and stop managing your own browser infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;// Before: connecting to self-hosted Chrome\nconst browser = await puppeteer.connect({\n  browserWSEndpoint: &amp;#039;ws://localhost:9222/devtools/browser&amp;#039;\n});\n\n// After: connecting to Browser Run\nconst browser = await puppeteer.connect({\n  browserWSEndpoint: &amp;#039;wss://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/&amp;lt;ACCOUNT_ID&amp;gt;/browser-rendering/devtools/browser&amp;#039;,\n  headers: { &amp;#039;Authorization&amp;#039;: &amp;#039;Bearer &amp;lt;API_TOKEN&amp;gt;&amp;#039; }\n});\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;The CDP endpoint also makes Browser Run more accessible. You can now connect from any language, any environment, without needing to write a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Worker&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. (If you&amp;#39;re already using Workers, nothing changes.)&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;using-browser-run-with-mcp-clients\&quot;&gt;Using Browser Run with MCP Clients&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#using-browser-run-with-mcp-clients\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Now that Browser Run exposes the Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP), MCP clients including Claude Desktop, Cursor, Codex, and OpenCode can use Browser Run as their remote browser. The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;chrome-devtools-mcp package&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; from the Chrome DevTools team is an MCP server that gives your AI coding assistant access to the full power of Chrome DevTools for reliable automation, in-depth debugging, and performance analysis.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Hereâs an example of how to configure Browser Run for Claude Desktop:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;{\n  &amp;quot;mcpServers&amp;quot;: {\n    &amp;quot;browser-rendering&amp;quot;: {\n      &amp;quot;command&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;npx&amp;quot;,\n      &amp;quot;args&amp;quot;: [\n        &amp;quot;-y&amp;quot;,\n        &amp;quot;chrome-devtools-mcp@latest&amp;quot;,\n        &amp;quot;--wsEndpoint=wss://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/&amp;lt;ACCOUNT_ID&amp;gt;/browser-rendering/devtools/browser?keep_alive=600000&amp;quot;,\n        &amp;quot;--wsHeaders={\\&amp;quot;Authorization\\&amp;quot;:\\&amp;quot;Bearer &amp;lt;API_TOKEN&amp;gt;\\&amp;quot;}&amp;quot;\n      ]\n    }\n  }\n}\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;For other MCP clients, see &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/cdp/mcp-clients/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;documentation for using Browser Run with MCP clients&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;webmcp-support\&quot;&gt;WebMCP support&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#webmcp-support\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Internet was built for humans, so navigating as an AI agent today is unreliable. Weâre betting on a future where more agents use the web than humans. In that world, sites need to be agent-friendly.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Thatâs why weâre launching support for &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developer.chrome.com/blog/webmcp-epp\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;WebMCP&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, a new browser API from the Google Chrome team that landed in Chromium 146+. WebMCP lets websites expose tools directly to AI agents, declaring what actions are available for agents to discover and call on each page. This helps agents navigate the web more reliably. Instead of agents needing to figure out how to use a site, websites can expose their tools for agents to discover and call&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Two APIs make this work:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;navigator.modelContext&lt;/code&gt; allows websites to register their tools&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;navigator.modelContextTesting&lt;/code&gt; allows agents to discover and execute those tools&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, an agent visiting a travel booking site has to figure out the UI by looking at it. With WebMCP, the site declares âhereâs a search_flights tool that takes an origin, destination, and date.â The agent calls it directly, without having to loop through slow screenshot-analyze-click loops. This makes navigation more reliable regardless of potential changes to the UI.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Tools are discovered on the page rather than preloaded. This matters for the long tail of the web, where preloading an MCP server for every possible site is not feasible and would bloat the context window.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;div style=\&quot;position: relative; padding-top: 62.7177700348432%;\&quot;&gt;\n  &lt;iframe\n    src=\&quot;https://customer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com/cdef25a5448e8f745c319974849a74d9/iframe?preload=true&amp;poster=https%3A%2F%2Fcustomer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com%2Fcdef25a5448e8f745c319974849a74d9%2Fthumbnails%2Fthumbnail.jpg%3Ftime%3D%26height%3D600\&quot;\n    loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;\n    style=\&quot;border: none; position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0; height: 100%; width: 100%;\&quot;\n    allow=\&quot;accelerometer; gyroscope; autoplay; encrypted-media; picture-in-picture;\&quot;\n    allowfullscreen=\&quot;true\&quot;\n  &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt;\n&lt;/div&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Using WebMCP to book a hotel through the Chrome DevTools console, discovering available tools with listTools()&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We have an experimental pool with browser instances running Chrome beta so you can test emerging browser features before they reach stable Chrome. We also just shipped &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/reference/wrangler-commands/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Wrangler browser commands&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that let you manage browser sessions directly from the CLI, letting you create, manage, and view browser sessions directly from your terminal. To &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-run/features/webmcp/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;access WebMCP-enabled browsers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, use the following Wrangler command to create a session in the experimental pool:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;npm i -g wrangler@latest\nwrangler browser create --lab --keepAlive 300  \n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            \n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;existing-ways-to-use-browser-run\&quot;&gt;Existing ways to use Browser Run&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#existing-ways-to-use-browser-run\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;While CDP and WebMCP are new, you could already use &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/puppeteer/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Puppeteer&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/playwright/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Playwright&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, or &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/stagehand/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Stagehand&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for full browser automation through Browser Run. And for simple tasks like &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/screenshot-endpoint/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;capturing screenshots&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/pdf-endpoint/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;generating PDFs&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/markdown-endpoint/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;/crawl endpoint&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that lets you crawl entire sites with a single API call. Give it a starting URL and pages are automatically discovered and scraped, then returned in your preferred format (HTML, Markdown, and structured JSON), with additional parameters to control crawl depth and scope, skip pages that havenât changed, and specify certain paths to include or exclude.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We intentionally built /crawl to be a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-run/faq/#will-browser-run-be-detected-by-bot-management\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;well-behaved crawler&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. That means it respects site ownerâs preferences out of the box, is a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/concepts/bot/signed-agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;signed agent&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; with a distinct bot ID that is cryptographically signed using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/reference/bot-verification/web-bot-auth/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Web Bot Auth&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, a non-customizable &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/crawl-endpoint/#user-agent\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;User-Agent&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and follows robots.txt and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/ai-crawl-control/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AI Crawl Control&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. It does not bypass Cloudflareâs bot protections or CAPTCHAs. Site owners choose whether their content is accessible and /crawl respects it.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;# Initiate a crawl\ncurl -X POST &amp;#039;https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/{account_id}/browser-rendering/crawl&amp;#039; \\\n  -H &amp;#039;Authorization: Bearer &amp;lt;apiToken&amp;gt;&amp;#039; \\\n  -H &amp;#039;Content-Type: application/json&amp;#039; \\\n  -d &amp;#039;{\n    &amp;quot;url&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/&amp;quot;\n  }&amp;#039;\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            \n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;3-observe\&quot;&gt;3) Observe&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#3-observe\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Things donât always go right the first try. Using sandboxed TypeScript, it helps you troubleshoot and manage your stack as a grounded technical collaborator.\n&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;While there have been small improvements along the way, the interface of technical products has not really changed since the dawn of the Internet. It still remains: clicking five pages deep, cross-referencing logs across tabs, and hunting for hidden toggles.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;AI gives us the opportunity to rethink all that. Instead of complexity spread over a sprawling graphical user interface: what if you could describe in plain language what you wanted to achieve?Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is the future â and weâre launching it today. We didnât want to just put an agent in a dashboard. We wanted to create an entirely new way to interact with our entire platform. Any task, any surface, a single prompt.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Introducing Agent Lee.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Agent Lee is an in-dashboard AI assistant that understands &lt;b&gt;your&lt;/b&gt; Cloudflare account.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;It can help you with troubleshooting, which, today, is a manual grind. If your Worker starts returning 503s at 02:00 UTC, finding the root cause:Â be it an R2 bucket, a misconfigured route, or a hidden rate limit, youâre opening half a dozen tabs and hoping you recognize the pattern. Most developers don&amp;#39;t have a teammate who knows the entire platform standing over their shoulder at 2 a.m. Agent Lee does.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But it wonât just troubleshoot for you at 2 a.m. Agent Lee will also fix the problem for you on the spot.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2Iva79HIiHPUrK8NLukkwH/dd1cf1709ab04f6d5825124cecd20a5e/BLOG-3231_2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3231 2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1013\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Agent Lee has been running in an active beta during which it has served over 18,000 daily users, executing nearly a quarter of a million tool calls per day. While we are confident in its current capabilities and success in production, this is a system we are continuously developing. As it remains in beta, you may encounter unexpected limitations or edge cases as we refine its performance. We encourage you to use the feedback form below to help us make it better every day.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-agent-lee-can-do\&quot;&gt;What Agent Lee can do&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-agent-lee-can-do\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agent Lee is built directly into the dashboard and understands the resources in your account. It knows your Workers, your zones, your DNS configuration, your error rates. The knowledge that today lives across six tabs and two browser windows will now live in one place, and you can talk to it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;With natural language, you can use it to:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Answer questions about your account:&lt;/b&gt; &amp;quot;Show me the top 5 error messages on my Worker.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Debug an issue:&lt;/b&gt; &amp;quot;I can&amp;#39;t access my site with the www prefix.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Apply a change:&lt;/b&gt; &amp;quot;Enable Access for my domain.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Deploy a resource: &lt;/b&gt;&amp;quot;Create a new R2 bucket for my photos and connect it to my Worker.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Instead of switching between products, you describe what you want to do, and Agent Lee helps you get there with instructions and visualizations. It retrieves context, uses the right tools, and creates dynamic visualizations based on the types of questions you ask. Ask what your error rate looks like over the last 24 hours, and it renders a chart inline, pulling from your actual traffic, not sending you to a separate Analytics page.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div style=\&quot;position: relative; padding-top: 44.08713692946058%;\&quot;&gt;\n  &lt;iframe\n    src=\&quot;https://customer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com/16f8e9c688d9493c1ddb124e0d597d43/iframe?poster=https%3A%2F%2Fcustomer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com%2F16f8e9c688d9493c1ddb124e0d597d43%2Fthumbnails%2Fthumbnail.jpg%3Ftime%3D%26height%3D600\&quot;\n    loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;\n    style=\&quot;border: none; position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0; height: 100%; width: 100%;\&quot;\n    allow=\&quot;accelerometer; gyroscope; autoplay; encrypted-media; picture-in-picture;\&quot;\n    allowfullscreen=\&quot;true\&quot;\n  &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt;\n&lt;/div&gt;&lt;p&gt;Agent Lee isn&amp;#39;t answering FAQ questions â it&amp;#39;s doing real work, against real accounts, at scale. aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Rather than presenting MCP tool definitions directly to the model, Agent Lee uses &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Codemode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to convert the tools into a TypeScript API and asks the model to write code that calls it instead.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This works better for a couple of reasons. LLMs have seen a huge amount of real-world TypeScript but very few tool call examples, so they&amp;#39;re more accurate when working in code. For multi-step tasks, the model can also chain calls together in a single script and return only the final result, ultimately skipping the round-trips.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The generated code is sent to an upstream Cloudflare MCP server for sandboxed execution, but it goes through a Durable Object that acts as a credentialed proxy. Before any call goes out, the DO classifies the generated code as read or write by inspecting the method and body. Read operations are proxied directly. Write operations are blocked until you explicitly approve them through the elicitation gate. API keys are never present in the generated code â they&amp;#39;re held inside the DO and injected server-side when the upstream call is made. The security boundary isn&amp;#39;t just a sandbox that gets thrown away; it&amp;#39;s a permission architecture that structurally prevents writes from happening without your approval.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-mcp-permission-system\&quot;&gt;The MCP permission system&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-mcp-permission-system\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agent Lee connects to Cloudflare&amp;#39;s own MCP server, which exposes two tools: a search tool for querying API endpoints and an execute tool for writing code that performs API requests. This is the surface through which Agent Lee reads your account and, when you approve, writes to it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Write operations go through an elicitation system that surfaces the approval step before any code executes. Agent Lee cannot skip this step. The permission model is the enforcement layer, and the confirmation prompt you see is not a UX courtesy. It&amp;#39;s the gate.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/s8phQottGj8yVgc42Nzvl/3abb536756e10360b68cabc0522bcb30/BLOG-3231_4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3231 4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1608\&quot; height=\&quot;1378\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;built-on-the-same-stack-you-can-use\&quot;&gt;Built on the same stack you can use&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#built-on-the-same-stack-you-can-use\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Every primitive Agent Lee is built on is available to all our customers: &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents SDK&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/products/workers-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers AI&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/products/durable-objects/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and the same MCP infrastructure available to any Cloudflare developer. We didn&amp;#39;t build internal tools that aren&amp;#39;t available to you â instead we built it with the same Cloudflare lego blocks that you have access to.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Building Agent Lee on our own primitives wasn&amp;#39;t just a design principle. It was the fastest way to find out what works and what doesn&amp;#39;t. We built this in production, with real users, against real accounts. That means every limitation we hit is a limitation we can fix in the platform. Every pattern that works is one we can make easier for the next team that builds on top of it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These are not opinions. They&amp;#39;re what quarter of a million tool calls across 18,000 users a day are telling us.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6877BA4kZUUP6qTs5ONucr/50d572b38fecdb3e77ab38d7976f06ed/image5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1135\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;generative-ui\&quot;&gt;Generative UI&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#generative-ui\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Interacting with a platform should feel like collaborating with an expert. Conversations should transcend simple text. With Agent Lee, as your dialogue evolves, the platform dynamically generates UI components alongside textual responses to provide a richer, more actionable experience.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For example, if you ask about website traffic trends for the month, you wonât just get a paragraph of numbers. Agent Lee will render an interactive line graph, allowing you to visualize peaks and troughs in activity at a glance.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To give you full creative control, every conversation is accompanied within an adaptive grid. Here you can click and drag across the grid to carve out space for new UI blocks, then simply describe what you want to see and let the agent handle the heavy lifting.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, we support a diverse library of visual blocks, including dynamic tables, interactive charts, architecture maps, and more. By blending the flexibility of natural language with the clarity of structured UI, Agent Lee transforms your chat history into a living dashboard.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1oTLXzK5eYGyJm6Y54cKbz/98bae92dc7523f63ac6515d8088e70f7/image4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1032\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;measuring-quality-and-safety\&quot;&gt;Measuring quality and safety&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#measuring-quality-and-safety\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;An agent that can take action on your account needs to be reliable and secure. Elicitations allow agentic systems to actively solicit information, preferences, or approvals from users or other systems mid-execution. When Agent Lee needs to take non-read actions on a user&amp;#39;s behalf we use elicitations by requiring an explicit approval action in the user interface. These guardrails allow Agent Lee to truly be a partner alongside you in managing your resource safely.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In addition to safety, we continuously measure quality.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Evals to measure conversation success rate and information accuracy.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Feedback signals from user interactions (thumbs up / thumbs down).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Tool call execution success rate and hallucination scorers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Per-product breakdown of conversation performance.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;These systems help us improve Agent Lee over time while keeping users in control.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;our-vision-ahead\&quot;&gt;Our vision ahead&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#our-vision-ahead\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agent Lee in the dashboard is only the beginning.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The bigger vision is Agent Lee as the interface to the entire Cloudflare platform â from anywhere. The dashboard today, the CLI next, your phone when you&amp;#39;re on the go. The surface you use shouldn&amp;#39;t matter. You should be able to describe what you need and have it done, regardless of where you are.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;From there, Agent Lee gets proactive. Rather than waiting to be asked, it watches what matters to you, your Workers, your traffic, your error thresholds and reaches out when something warrants attention. An agent that only responds is useful. One that notices things first is something different.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Underlying all of this is context. Agent Lee already knows your account configuration. Over time, it will know more, what you&amp;#39;ve asked before, what page you&amp;#39;re on, what you were debugging last week. That accumulated context is what makes a platform feel less like a tool and more like a collaborator.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We&amp;#39;re not there yet. Agent Lee today is the first step, running in production, doing real work at scale. The architecture is built to get to the rest. &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;try-it-out\&quot;&gt;Try it out&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#try-it-out\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agent Lee is available in beta for Free plan users.
